All requested files were read in full via `mcp__jarvis__vault_read`. Below is exact evidence, organized by source path, followed by synthesized answers to your six questions.

## Source-by-source notes

**`20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Claude Kit/Tool Map.md`** (type: index, status: active, created 2026-07-29, updated 2026-07-30, tags: claude-code/ai-use/ingestion/claude-kit)
- Explicit self-description: "This is the living map of everything ingested into `gupta-builds/second-brain-claudekit`... one row per tool, updated the same session its pipeline stage changes."
- Frontmatter `next` field literally says: "Add a row here the same session anything new lands in second-brain-claudekit's sandbox/, tested-skills/, or a rigid folder — see that repo's Docs/Jarvis.md for the ritual." (Note: it says `Docs/Jarvis.md`, not `_docs/Jarvis.md` — a naming mismatch worth flagging for your doc rewrite since your repo currently uses `_docs/`.)
- States it tracks a *different layer* than `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/&lt;Project&gt;/Setup.md` (which are per-project deployed-`.claude/` inventories).
- **Pipeline stages, defined here**: `sandbox` → `tested-skills` → `promoted (repo-scoped)` / `promoted (global)`, or `blocked`/`dropped`/`undecided` at any point. (Note: this note uses `tested-skills`, but other notes below use `tested-tools` — inconsistent naming across the vault itself, both referring to your repo's `tested-tools/` folder.)
- Contains real per-tool rows: GBrain (global candidate, sandbox stage, blocked on embedding-provider decision), gstack (blocked, missing libnss3.so), mattpocock-skills engineering category (`tested-skills/mattpocock-engineering/`), ECC (sandbox/ecc/, 3378/3388 tests passed), plus 17 clone-only repos added 2026-07-30.
- Real finding logged here: cloning ECC into `sandbox/` caused Claude Code to auto-load its `CLAUDE.md`/rules/skills — flagged as breaking `Docs/Architecture.md`'s assumption that sandbox is inert until run.

**`20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Claude Kit/Log.md`** (no frontmatter type/tags — plain content, dated entries)
- Heading: "Dated entries, one per addition or status change in `second-brain-claudekit`'s tool pipeline. Same heading convention as ... the main Session Log (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] tag | title`)."
- Confirms this file is real and actively used — dense entries from 2026-07-29 through 2026-08-11 covering sandbox clones, sync tooling (Unison), ECC testing, conversation-capture rebuild.
- **This is genuinely the tool-pipeline changelog your repo's docs claim it is** — it is not aspirational, it has real dated entries with concrete commands run and results.
- One important correction embedded in the log itself (2026-07-30 entry): a prior 2026-07-29 note wrongly called `ecc2` an "unrelated Rust project"; this was reverted after checking `git remote -v`.

**Agents/Commands/Hooks/MCPs/Skills — the "How to Use X" / "What X" pattern** (all created 2026-08-10, all `type: evergreen`, `status: sprout`)

Every category consistently has exactly two files with a fixed job split, stated explicitly in `Toolkit/Claude Code.md`:
&gt; "`What {Category}.md` is a ground-truth inventory — split into what second-brain-claudekit itself has promoted into its own `.claude/`... and what is actually live in Jarvis's real vault-root `.claude/` right now, verified against the files on disk, not against a stale snapshot. `How to Use {Category}.md` is the dispatch note — a `# Claude Kit` section giving project-agnostic usage guidance per promoted claudekit tool, and a `# Particular Use` section of named-use-case anchors ... that the use-case notes below link straight into."

Concretely, each `What X.md` has two headings: `## Promoted in claudekit` and `## Live in Jarvis`. Each `How to Use X.md` has two headings: `# Claude Kit` (a paragraph or bullet list giving generic guidance per promoted claudekit tool) and `# Particular Use` (subheadings per named use-case, e.g. `## Research &amp; Distillation`, each linking to a shared use-case note like `[[Research &amp; Distillation]]`).

Per-category specifics found:
- **Agents/What Agents.md**: claudekit's `.claude/agents/` has 3 (vault-curator, research-distiller, weekly-reviewer), unchanged since scaffold commit `d35f0b7` (2026-04-03) — "none came through the sandbox → tested-tools → promoted pipeline; they predate that pipeline entirely." A separate top-level `agents/` folder in the repo (`connector.md`, `researcher.md`, `reviewer.md`, `writer.md`) is called out explicitly as "a staging area for drafts, not promoted content." Jarvis itself has 5 agents, independently built despite name overlap.
- **Commands/What Commands.md**: claudekit's `.claude/commands/` has 11 files (context, today, trace, graduate, closeday, emerge, ghost, challenge, ideas, drift, schedule) — "all from the original scaffold." A separate top-level `commands/` folder (11 different files: preserve, compress, resume, capture, brainstorm, connect, research, review, summarize, inbox-process, journal) is explicitly called "the repo's *global* command set, meant to be copied to `~/.claude/commands/` — not yet copied anywhere, still repo-local." Jarvis has 19 live commands, 4 name-collide with claudekit's set independently.
- **Hooks/What Hooks.md**: claudekit's two hooks (after-edit-log.ps1, session-wrapup.ps1) both wired in its settings.json. Notes a real incident: hardcoded pre-rename path caused a folder to silently recreate itself after a git restore, fixed 2026-08-08, referenced as `_docs/Repo-Map.md`'s "50_Claude recreation bug." Jarvis side: only 1 of 2 documented hooks actually registered (jarvis-write-guard.ps1); jarvis-session-continuity.ps1 exists on disk but is NOT in settings.json — flagged as a real gap, not documentation nitpick.
- **MCPs/What MCPs.md**: "None" promoted in claudekit. GBrain is the only candidate, still sandbox stage. Notably states: `second-brain-claudekit/_docs/Current-Setup.md` lists MCP servers connected in a live session (github, jarvis, jarvis-fs, the-plan, the-plan-fs, graphify, pencil) — explicitly says these "are session-level connections, not artifacts that cleared the qualification pipeline, and are not 'promoted' in the pipeline sense this note tracks." Jarvis's real `.mcp.json` has 6 servers.
- **Skills/What Skills.md**: "None. `skills/` at the repo's top level (the staging area for drafts) is empty, confirmed by direct listing. Nothing has reached the skill-authoring stage of the pipeline yet — every promoted artifact in that repo so far is an agent, command, or hook." **`Toolkit/Skills/Github Skills.md`** (pre-existing note, created 2026-06-21, `type: evergreen`, `status: sprout`, tags: `cs` only — does NOT follow the claude-kit tagging convention of the 2026-08-10 batch) — explicitly called out in `Claude Code.md` as "the one pre-existing note in this folder that was already real and stays as-is." **`Toolkit/Claude Code.md`** (the MOC/index for the whole Toolkit folder, `type: index`, `status: sprout`, tags: moc/claude-kit/toolkit)
- Defines the Toolkit's purpose precisely: "The Toolkit is the reference layer between Tool Map.md (which tracks pipeline *stage*...) and an actual working session. Tool Map answers 'is this tool trustworthy yet'; the Toolkit answers 'given a real task right now, what do I actually type.'"

# Review System — Complete Picture from Jarvis (Research Report)

## Critical finding: `10_Areas/AI/Setup/Review System.md` is not "vague" — it is a completely empty file (0 bytes, empty frontmatter, empty content). All of the actual design work happened in a conversation log, not in that note. That note has zero content to build from; the repo's `_docs/` needs to pull from the conversation log below, not from that stub.

## Primary source: the conversation log

`60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Windows/Claude Code/Jarvis/08-10 Plan second-brain-claudekit workflow and review system.md` (Windows Claude Code session, `started_at: 2026-08-10T15:02:31`, `ended_at: 2026-08-11T14:08:45`, 509KB raw transcript) is where the review system was actually designed and partially built. This is the load-bearing source. Key facts, with verbatim quotes:

### Decision: where the review system lives (settled, not open)
An `AskUserQuestion` in the session posed: *"Where should the automated tool/skill-usage review system live?"* with options (a) build out `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/` as its own tree, (b) fold into the existing `/weekly-review` skill and Weekly Synthesis Index, (c) something else. The user chose (a). This is confirmed both in the transcript and in `10_Areas/AI/Setup/Gaps.md`'s frontmatter diff:

&gt; "Decided 2026-08-10: the automated review system builds out under `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/` as its own tree, kept separate from `Weekly Synthesis/`/`Monthly/` since that structure is concept-mastery review (Capability Engine), a different subject — not yet built, but no longer an open question."

### What was actually built that session (not vague — concretely shipped)
1. **`30_Order/Standards/Review Standard.md`** (full content read) — the governing content standard for `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/{Weekly,Monthly}/` reviews. Verbatim opening: *"A review cites the actual log rows it read — Tool log, Sync-Log, Write Log — it never summarizes from memory or impression."* It explicitly states its subject is **usage/sync health**, distinct from `Weekly Synthesis Template.md`/`Monthly Synthesis Template.md`, which cover concept mastery (Capability Engine) — "do not conflate the two folders or reuse one template for the other's job."
   - **`Used By Workflow`** section, verbatim: *"Manual, human-triggered — no cron job writes a review. A review is produced by opening the relevant template and working through it against the real logs, the same session or shortly after the period it covers. Nothing here auto-generates from the logs; a human (or an agent explicitly asked to) reads them and writes the review."* — **this directly answers "what triggers it": nothing automatic. The review itself is a manual/human-triggered act.**
   - Required sections per the Standard: `Period Covered`, `Sources Reviewed` (must name every log actually opened — Tool log, `_All-Projects-Sync-Log`, `Write Log`, raw session notes), `What Ran This Period`, `Sync &amp; Capture Health` (real OK/failed counts, no vague claims), `Findings`, `Decided Fixes`, `Open Questions`, `Next Period's Watch List`.
   - **`Decided Fixes`** gate, verbatim: *"Only items where the fix is unambiguous and the review has 100% clarity on what happened... a review surfacing a problem is not itself authorization to auto-fix it. An item lands here only when the reviewer is certain, and even then the fix is applied by hand or flagged for the next build session — never by an automated process this review triggers."* This is the "100%-clarity gate before auto-fixes" the user asked for — it's a **documentation/discipline gate**, not a code-enforced mechanism. Nothing mechanically prevents an agent from acting without it; it's a written rule in the Standard.
   - `Done Conditions`: every claim in Sync &amp; Capture Health must trace to a real log row (cited by file/date/line); Decided Fixes only holds 100%-clarity items; standard vault formatting rules (no `---` in body, zero blank lines except after callouts, no duplicate frontmatter keys, passes `HUMAN_WRITING` test).
   - `Gold Standard Example`: **none yet** — "the first real Weekly review against this Standard becomes the example once written." No review has actually been written under this Standard.

2. **`30_Order/Templates/Capability/AI Tools Weekly Review Template.md`** (full content read, Templater-driven) — sections: Period Covered, Sources Reviewed (checkbox list: Tool log, `_All-Projects-Sync-Log`, Write Log, raw session notes "only if the Tool log flags something"), What Ran This Period (table: Skill/Command | Uses | Notes), Sync &amp; Capture Health (`Sync runs this period: OK ___ / failed ___ / skipped ___`, `Sessions captured vs. real sessions run (spot-check): ___`), Findings, Decided Fixes (gated per Review Standard), Open Questions, Next Period's Watch List.

3. **`30_Order/Templates/Capability/AI Tools Monthly Review Template.md`** (full content read) — same shape plus: Sources Reviewed adds "all four Weekly reviews from this month" and `Tool Map`; adds a **Tool Map Health Check** section — *"Cross-check Tool Map — anything stuck at `sandbox` or `tested-tools` for more than one full month gets named here, not silently carried forward again."*

4. **`60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/Tool log.md`** (read directly — this is the one file that exists in `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/`, per `vault_list`). Full frontmatter/content:

Filed under `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/`, not `05_Clippings/`, on purpose — this is a derived, continuously-updated index built from raw captures, not a raw capture itself, and `05_Clippings` is read-only after capture per the vault's Write Contract (`AGENTS.md`)."*
   - It was **relocated mid-session**: the session log records *"The Write Contract hook just blocked me — `60_Claude/05_Clippings` is read-only after capture (enforced, not a suggestion). Tool log is a continuously-updated index... belongs in the already-designated review scaffold instead: `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/`."* An earlier empty placeholder was left at `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Windows/Claude Code/Tool log.md` and should be deleted (noted in the file itself) — not mistaken for the real one.
   - Row shape: `| Date | Project | Skill/Command | What It Did | Outcome | Source |` — one row per skill/command invocation per session (a skill used twice in a session = two rows). `Outcome` is never blank. `Source` links the raw session note, not the distilled summary.
   - **The log table is currently empty** — `next:` frontmatter says "First real rows land the next time `/export-ai-session` runs against Windows or WSL Claude Code candidates." Nothing has been logged yet.
   - Written by: `/export-ai-session` skill only (`C:\Users\Anant Gupta\.claude\skills\export-ai-session\SKILL.md`) — never hand-edited.

5. **`export-ai-session` skill was rewritten this session** (full content read via the transcript, which contains a `Read` of the skill file). Its documented **capture-layer architecture** ("The layers"), verbatim structure:
   - **Layer 0 — Raw JSONL mirror** (Claude Code main only, **zero token**): `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Windows/Claude Code/_raw_jsonl/` (NTFS junction) and a WSL one-way-copy equivalent. Complete, unredacted, gitignored. **This is the actual mechanism that achieves "0 token cost"** — it's a filesystem mirror/junction, not an LLM call.
   - **Layer 1 — Raw archive note** (automatic, **~0 tokens**): one markdown file per session, written by PowerShell hook scripts (`export-claude-session.ps1` on Windows, `wsl-session-export.ps1` on WSL) triggered by the `Stop` hook (now, as of this session — see below) and `SessionEnd`. Immutable once a session truly ends; live-updated in place while still running. Frontmatter carries `tools_used`, `tokens`, `cost_usd`, `files_touched`; body has a **Tool Calls** section with real per-turn arguments. Best-effort redaction of key-shaped strings.
   - **Layer 2 — Distilled summary** (`/export-ai-session` skill, **costs tokens**, requires an LLM to run and requires user confirmation of which sessions to distill via `AskUserQuestion`): written to `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/AI Conversation - Summaries/Claude Code/` or `.../Cowork/`. As of 2026-08-11 this now includes a required **Tool Use Detail** section — a narrative (not a re-list) of which tools/skills ran, on what inputs, grouped by purpose.
   - **Layer 3 — Tool log entry** (skill's tier 3, cheap): one row per session reviewed appended to the Tool log described above, regardless of whether a full distilled summary was written.
   - **Layer 4 — Promotion**: "separate, manual, later decision. Never automatic."
   - **So "0 token cost" concretely means**: the *capture* (layers 0–1) is free — it's PowerShell hooks + filesystem mirroring, no LLM involved. The *review* (reading logs and writing a Weekly/Monthly review per the Standard) is explicitly manual/human-triggered and does cost tokens when an agent does it. Nothing in the design claims the review itself is free — only that raw capture is.

6. **Stop hook wiring** (this session's real infra change, confirmed in the summary): *"`Stop` now triggers export on both platforms (fires after every turn, far more reliable than `SessionEnd` alone), with a 30-minute scheduled `-BackfillAll` safety net on each, staggered 15 minutes apart."* Both a Windows and WSL `Stop`-hook export were added, plus scheduled Windows Task Scheduler entries `ConversationCapture-Backfill-Windows` / `-WSL` (hidden, honest exit code) as a catch-all safety net for anything the hooks missed. These are registered as real Windows Scheduled Tasks and were verified (`LastTaskResult: 0`) at session's end.
   - Note: this Stop/SessionEnd hook wiring is the **conversation-capture** scheduled task, not a scheduled task for *running the review itself*. No cron/task exists for writing a Weekly/Monthly review — see below.

7. **No scheduled task exists for the review-writing step.** `10_Areas/AI/Setup/Gaps.md` states directly: *"Schedule the weekly/monthly cadence via `CronCreate` or the `schedule` skill once the review-writing logic exists — `CronList` currently returns no scheduled jobs at all."* And `Review Standard.md` itself says "no cron job writes a review" — this is by design, not an oversight; review is meant to be manual or explicitly agent-triggered.

## WSL vs Windows conversation-logging — confirmed status and root cause

From `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/AI Conversation - Summaries/WSL Claude Code — Wiring Gap.md` (created 2026-07-29, **now superseded/fixed** by the 08-10/08-11 session — treat this note as history, not current state):
- Root cause identified: WSL runs a **completely separate** Claude Code instance with its own `~/.claude/projects` and `~/.claude/settings.json`. That settings file had its own hook wiring (`after-edit-log.ps1`, `session-wrapup.ps1` via `pwsh`) but **not** `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1` — so WSL sessions vanished with no vault trace.
- The proposed fix (adapt the continuity script for WSL paths, register as a third `SessionEnd` hook entry, extend `/export-ai-session`'s candidate search) is exactly what got implemented in the 08-10/08-11 session.
- **Current status per the 08-10/08-11 session's closing summary**: *"Conversation capture — rebuilt, not patched. `Stop` now triggers export on both platforms... Windows and WSL Claude Code are both live; WSL raw notes now land in `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/&lt;project&gt;/` the same way Windows notes do — this skill's candidate search covers both."* A real bug was hit and fixed along the way: the first fix attempt created 45 duplicate notes (10 Windows, 35 WSL) from old markers with no stored filename to reuse; this was caught, root-caused, the duplicates deleted, and idempotency re-verified on both platforms.
- Also fixed in the same pass: a Windows title-extraction bug (sessions opening with `/startday`-style commands got mistitled as generic "Base directory for this skill" because the exporter's title logic skipped any turn starting with `&lt;` to dodge `&lt;local-command-caveat&gt;` wrapper text, but a directory-skill's own preamble doesn't start with `&lt;`).
- Important scope clarification the user gave mid-session (direct quote): *"To correct you about wsl, I did not mean jarvis in wsl. I meant that any session inside wsl was not syncing. Especially the sessions that were with second-brain-claudekit. There is no jarvis in wsl there are other repositories inside wsl that use claude."* — i.e., the WSL gap specifically mattered for **second-brain-claudekit** sessions (this very repo), not a WSL copy of the Jarvis vault.
- The Cowork sweep (separate from Claude Code) is gated to `SessionEnd` only, not `Stop`, since it doesn't need per-turn firing.

`10_Areas/AI/Setup/Folder Map.md`'s older framing (pre-fix) had described WSL capture and Cowork capture as both "stalled" (WSL since 2026-07-30, Cowork since 2026-07-24) with "cause not visible from vault files alone." That diagnosis is now resolved by the 08-10/08-11 session for WSL Claude Code specifically; Cowork's status wasn't touched in that session beyond being scoped to `SessionEnd` only.

## Ground-Truth Report: AI Conversation Logging System (as of 2026-08-19)

### 1. Date coverage — WSL vs Windows

**WSL (`60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/`)** — 8 project folders (`CausalOps`, `ecc`, `everything-claude-code`, `gupta-builds`, `internship-research-loop`, `portfolio`, `second-brain-claudekit`, `tradingview`; `ecc` and `everything-claude-code` are empty scaffolds). Across **every** WSL project, the newest dated file is **`second-brain-claudekit/08-08 Establish folder structure and catalog codebase details.md`**. Its frontmatter shows `started_at: 2026-08-08T17:24:29`, `ended_at: 2026-08-11T08:28:03` (a 63-hour spanning session), `exported_at: 2026-08-12T13:15:10` — i.e. the file is named for its start date but the underlying export activity landed 2026-08-12. **Nothing on the WSL side has a start date, end date, or export date after 2026-08-12.** `internship-research-loop` stalls at 08-03, `tradingview` at 07-21, `portfolio` at 07-24, `CausalOps` at 07-09, `gupta-builds` at 06-30.

**Windows (`.../Windows/Claude Code/`)** — folders `Home` (empty, 0 dated notes — confirms the old cwd-allowlist bug is gone but nothing has run there), `Jarvis` (by far the busiest — 50 dated notes), `The Plan` (empty). Newest file: **`Jarvis/08-10 Plan second-brain-claudekit workflow and review system.md`**. Nothing after 08-10. `Windows/Cowork` has no `2026-08` month folder at all — its last content is `2026-07/07-24 [image attached].md`.

**Confirmed: "last week mostly blank" is accurate.** From 2026-08-12/08-13 through today, 2026-08-19 (7 days), there is **zero new capture on either OS**. From 2026-08-11 (Windows) / 08-08 start (WSL) backward to late July, coverage is denser but still gappy (e.g. Windows/Cowork gap since 07-24, `WSL/Claude Code/Jarvis` doesn't even exist as a folder).

### 2. Root cause — per `WSL Claude Code — Wiring Gap.md` and the actual fix session

The doc's original (2026-07-29) framing: WSL's `~/.claude/settings.json` never had the `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1`-equivalent `SessionEnd` hook wired at all — sessions "vanish with no vault trace." That was fixed 2026-07-30 (`wsl-session-export.ps1` added as a third WSL `SessionEnd` hook).

**Amendment (2026-08-11), added directly to `AI Conversations/README.md`:** the WSL exporter had been *silently dead since 2026-07-30* — 11 days, despite settings.json reporting no errors. Immediate cause: every hook (`after-edit-log.ps1`, `session-wrapup.ps1`, `wsl-session-export.ps1`) invoked `pwsh`, which didn't exist on `PATH`; a naive fix to `powershell.exe` "succeeded" at launching but silently broke the exporter because `powershell.exe` is a native Windows process that can't resolve the script's WSL/POSIX paths (`/mnt/d/...`, `$env:HOME`). Real fix: installed PowerShell 7.6.4 for Linux natively into WSL (`~/.local/bin/pwsh`), reverted all hooks to `pwsh`. Verified via a real `-BackfillAll` run: `second-brain-claudekit: seen=8, written=1, dup=7`.

**A second, deeper root cause was found the same session (documented in the raw transcript `Windows/Claude Code/Jarvis/08-10 Plan second-brain-claudekit workflow and review system.md`, heading "Conversation capture: found the real root cause..."):** manually running both exporters' `-BackfillAll` recovered sessions the hooks had missed even after the `pwsh` fix — 4 missed Windows sessions (incl. part of that very conversation, split by a compaction event) and 7 missed WSL sessions. Direct quote from that session: *"The actual root cause: capture depends entirely on SessionEnd firing, and it doesn't fire reliably for every real session-termination path (closing a terminal abruptly, machine sleep, WSL shutdown mid-session)... The gap is there's no scheduled safety net, unlike sync (which now has one) and unlike Cursor capture (which already uses a 15-minute scheduled sweep)."* The fix proposed — a scheduled `-BackfillAll` task per platform, mirroring the existing Cursor sweep and Unison sync pattern — was **explicitly not built**: *"I stopped short of registering it since this message says discussion, not build."*

That same transcript also clarifies `WSL/Claude Code/Jarvis/` doesn't exist because Claude Code has never actually been run from WSL with the Jarvis vault as cwd — not a capture bug.

**Is it confirmed working now?** Only as of the 2026-08-11/12 manual backfill. No scheduled safety net exists, so the system is back to depending solely on `SessionEnd` firing — the exact failure mode just diagnosed. The observed 7-9 day blank stretch since is consistent with that mechanism failing again (or simply not firing for whatever reason on some sessions) with nothing to catch it.

### 3. "0 token cost" mechanism vs. summarization

Two genuinely distinct mechanisms, correctly separated in the docs:
- **Capture (claimed 0-cost):** a `SessionEnd` PowerShell hook (`export-claude-session.ps1` on Windows, `wsl-session-export.ps1` on WSL) mirrors the session's own JSONL transcript and renders a markdown note — no LLM call involved, hence genuinely free. This is the layer that's currently broken/gapped, per above.
- **Summarization (not free, LLM-based):** the global `/export-ai-session` skill is supposed to review raw captures and, for "crucial/important sessions only," write a distilled summary into `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/AI Conversation - Summaries/`. This is a separate, deliberately-manual, LLM-cost step.

### 4. Summarization/distillation backlog

- `Summaries-to-Create.md` is **completely empty (0 bytes)** — no backlog has ever been written down there.
- The 4 existing `*Summary.md` files are all **Cursor** conversations, not Claude Code: dated 2026-05-28, 2026-06-04, 2026-07-05 (×2). **No Claude Code conversation has ever been distilled into a summary**, despite ~90 raw Claude Code session notes existing across Windows/WSL.
- `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/Tool log.md` (the per-skill-use index `/export-ai-session` is supposed to append to) was created 2026-08-11 and its `## Log` table is still **just the header row — zero entries**. `/export-ai-session` has apparently never run.

  + ### `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/` — raw conversation clippings

Organized by OS (`WSL/`, `Windows/`) then by tool (`Claude Code`, `Cursor`, `Kiro`, and `Cowork` under `Windows/`). Should be **live** for Claude Code and Cowork at minimum, likely Cursor too — the existing `_raw_jsonl` NTFS-junction pattern (documented in this folder's own `README.md`) already proves the mechanism for same-OS, read-only session-transcript mirroring; `_docs/Sync.md` is the record of what does and doesn't extend across the WSL↔Windows boundary.

**Ground-truth capture status as of 2026-08-19 (superseding the paragraph above, which described the intended design, not verified reality):** the capture layer has a real, two-part architecture — Layer 0 (a zero-token raw JSONL mirror, an NTFS junction on Windows / a one-way copy on WSL) and Layer 1 (an automatic, near-zero-token markdown archive note, written by a `Stop`/`SessionEnd`-triggered PowerShell hook — `export-claude-session.ps1` on Windows, `wsl-session-export.ps1` on WSL). This has broken and been re-fixed twice already: first, WSL never had the export hook wired at all until 2026-07-30; second, every hook silently died again for 11 days (2026-07-30 → 2026-08-11) because the hooks called `pwsh`, which wasn't on `PATH`, and a naive fallback to native `powershell.exe` "worked" but couldn't resolve WSL paths, so nothing was captured while `settings.json` reported no error. Both fixes are recorded in `AI Conversations/README.md`'s 2026-08-11 amendment.

**A third, deeper reliability gap was diagnosed 2026-08-10/11 and deliberately left unbuilt**, per the raw session transcript at `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Windows/Claude Code/Jarvis/08-10 Plan second-brain-claudekit workflow and review system.md`: capture depends entirely on `SessionEnd` firing, which doesn't fire reliably for every real termination path (an abruptly closed terminal, machine sleep, a WSL shutdown mid-session). The fix — a scheduled `-BackfillAll` safety net per platform, mirroring the pattern already proven for the Cursor sweep and the Unison vault sync — was explicitly scoped but not registered ("this message says discussion, not build").

**As of 2026-08-19, that unbuilt gap has already recurred**: nothing has been captured on either OS since 2026-08-10 (Windows) / 2026-08-12 (WSL) — a 7–9 day blank stretch confirmed by direct listing of every dated file in both `WSL/Claude Code/` and `Windows/Claude Code/`. "WSL is the weak link" is only half the story — this specific stall hit both platforms simultaneously, for the same undiagnosed-until-now-unbuilt reason. **Building the scheduled backfill safety net (Windows Task Scheduler + WSL cron, one per platform, same shape as the existing sync/Cursor sweeps) is the single highest-leverage fix available for this repo's own conversation-logging requirement** — see `_docs/Gaps.md`.

**Summarization (Layer 2, the `/export-ai-session` skill, genuinely token-costly, distinct from capture) has essentially never run against Claude Code sessions**: `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/AI Conversation - Summaries/` holds exactly 4 distilled summaries, all Cursor, none Claude Code, despite ~90 raw Claude Code session notes existing across both platforms. `Summaries-to-Create.md` (the intended backlog file) is empty. `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/Tool log.md` (Layer 3, the per-skill-use index `/export-ai-session` is supposed to append a row to on every run) exists with the correct schema but has zero data rows.

  - ### `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/` — the review layer

Reviews of AI conversations and tool use, in three subfolders: `Conversations/`, `Tools/`, and `Scheduled/` (split into `Monthly/` and `Weekly/`). Explicitly flagged as needing better sub-structure and, eventually, an automated review system that runs alongside automated skill/agent improvement: output from conversations is reviewed, tool use and skill use are measured in detail, a review is written, then a log entry records what happened.

**This automation does not exist yet.** Per the self-improvement sequencing this repo committed to 2026-08-09 (`_docs/Design.md`): the qualification pipeline runs solidly first, real evidence accumulates, only then does "what to automate" get decided — and any scheduled job eventually built for this review layer must write one visible, logged line per run, never act silently. **Only after 100% clarity on what a review found does an automated fix happen** — a review surfacing a problem is not itself authorization to act on it automatically.
  + ### `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/` — the review layer

Reviews of AI conversations and tool use, in subfolders `Conversations/`, `Tools/`, and `Scheduled/{Weekly,Monthly}/`.

**Status as of 2026-08-09 (`10_Areas/AI/Setup/Folder Map.md`, pre-dating the build below):** "a completely empty shell... nothing writes to it yet." That description is now **superseded** — real design and build work happened 2026-08-10/11, recorded in the raw session transcript at `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Windows/Claude Code/Jarvis/08-10 Plan second-brain-claudekit workflow and review system.md` (this session also settled where the system lives, via an explicit `AskUserQuestion`: its own tree under `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/`, kept deliberately separate from `Weekly Synthesis Index`/`60_Claude/30_Reviews/Monthly/`, which review a different subject — concept mastery, the Capability Engine — not usage/sync health).

**What's actually built (confirmed 2026-08-19, still current):**
- `30_Order/Standards/Review Standard.md` — the governing content standard. A review must cite the real log rows it read (Tool log, Sync-Log, Write Log) — "it never summarizes from memory or impression." Its `Used By Workflow` section states the trigger mechanism explicitly: **"Manual, human-triggered — no cron job writes a review."** This is a deliberate design decision, not a gap. Its `Decided Fixes` section is the literal 100%-clarity gate: *"a review surfacing a problem is not itself authorization to auto-fix it... even then the fix is applied by hand or flagged for the next build session — never by an automated process this review triggers."*
- `30_Order/Templates/Capability/AI Tools Weekly Review Template.md` and `.../AI Tools Monthly Review Template.md` — both real, Templater-driven, matching the Standard's required sections (Period Covered, Sources Reviewed, What Ran This Period, Sync & Capture Health, Findings, Decided Fixes, Open Questions, Next Period's Watch List; Monthly adds a Tool Map Health Check that names anything stuck at one pipeline stage for over a month).
- `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Tools/Tool log.md` — the real per-use index of skills/commands/agents across Windows and WSL, one row per invocation (`Date | Project | Skill/Command | What It Did | Outcome | Source`). Deliberately filed here, not in `05_Clippings/`, because the vault's Write Contract makes `05_Clippings/` read-only after capture and this file is continuously updated, not a raw capture. Written only by `/export-ai-session` — never hand-edited.

- No review has ever been written under the new Standard — its own "Gold Standard Example" field says "none yet."
- `Conversations/` is untouched by any of this build — still an empty, unwired subfolder with no defined purpose distinct from `Tools/`.
- No scheduling mechanism exists for the *review-writing step itself* — `CronList` returns nothing, and per the Standard this is by design (a human, or an agent explicitly asked, produces a review — nothing auto-generates one). What genuinely is missing is the *cadence* trigger (a reminder/cron that prompts the human/agent to sit down and write the next Weekly or Monthly review on schedule), which `10_Areas/AI/Setup/Gaps.md` still lists as open.

**Self-improvement sequencing still applies to anything beyond the above:** per `_docs/Design.md`'s 2026-08-09 commitment — the qualification pipeline runs solidly first, real evidence accumulates, only then does "what to automate further" get decided, and any future automation for this layer must write one visible, logged line per run, never act silently.

# Gaps — what's actually wrong in this repo right now

Written 2026-08-19, after a research-only pass across every note Anant pointed to in Jarvis (`20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Claude Kit/Toolkit/`, `20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Builds & Resources/`, `10_Areas/AI/Setup/`, `60_Claude/20_Distilled_Notes/Sources - Plan/`, `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/`, `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/`, `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/AI Conversation - Summaries/`) plus a direct audit of this repo's own `60_Claude/` folder. Mirrors Jarvis's own `10_Areas/AI/Setup/Gaps.md` convention on purpose — a plain, dated, no-spin list, updated as items close rather than rewritten from scratch. This doc does not propose fixes beyond naming them; nothing in `60_Claude/`, `instructions/`, `tests/`, or `tested-tools/_future/` was touched to produce it — read-only research, per instruction.

## 1. What's going wrong in `60_Claude/` right now

**The folder's own ground-truth doc had drifted stale inside itself.** `_docs/Repo-Map.md`'s folder-by-folder table (written 2026-08-08, "current as of 2026-08-08") said `scripts/` held 5 files. By 2026-08-10, two sessions later, the same doc's own "Open items" checklist recorded that a second, superseding sync engine (`sync-all.sh` + `sync-manifest.json` + `sync-all-silent.vbs` + `register-sync-task.ps1`) had been built and gone live — but the folder table itself was never revisited. Two true statements sat in the same file, one flatly contradicting the other, for 9 days, until this pass caught and fixed it. This is the exact "one fact, one home" failure Jarvis's own `Jarvis OS — North Star.md` principle exists to prevent, just recurring inside this repo's own docs instead of the vault.

**The repo has 10+ days of uncommitted work sitting in the working tree.** `git status` right now shows the same renames and deletions `_docs/Repo-Map.md` already documents as executed 2026-08-08/09 (`50_Claude/` deletions, `templates/`→`60_Claude/Templates/`, `vault-rules/`→`60_Claude/vault-rules/`, plus a batch of `.claude/` and top-level command/hook edits) — still uncommitted as of 2026-08-19. This lines up exactly with the conversation-capture gap found below: no session appears to have touched this repo's files between roughly 2026-08-09 and today. Not committed here without an explicit instruction; flagged as current, verified fact.

**This repo's own conversation-logging pipeline is stalled, not running.** This isn't a `60_Claude/` file problem directly — the capture mechanism lives in Jarvis-side/global hook config, not in this repo's `.claude/hooks/` — but it directly determines whether any of this repo's sessions get recorded at all. Per `_docs/Jarvis.md`'s updated "AI Conversations" section: nothing has been captured on either Windows or WSL since 2026-08-10/12, a 7–9 day blank stretch. The diagnosed root cause (`SessionEnd` doesn't fire reliably for every real session-termination path, and no scheduled backfill safety net exists to catch what it misses) was found and explicitly left unbuilt on 2026-08-11. It has now recurred exactly as predicted. **This is the single most consequential open gap for this repo's own "0 token cost, works at all times" requirement** — everything downstream (the Tool log, distilled summaries, the review system) depends on capture actually happening.

**`60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/` in Jarvis (the review layer this repo's docs point to) was real and built 2026-08-10/11, but this repo's own docs never said so.** Before this pass, `_docs/Jarvis.md` described it as "empty shell... automation does not exist yet" — true as of 2026-08-09, false by 2026-08-11. `_docs/Jarvis.md` is now corrected; see its "review layer" section for the concrete design (Review Standard, two templates, `Tool log.md`'s schema — deliberately manual/human-triggered by design, not a gap, but with zero data rows so far).

**Terminology drift between this repo and Jarvis was never reconciled.** Jarvis's own `Tool Map.md` — the doc both sides agree is the sole source of truth for tool-pipeline state — still calls the second stage `tested-skills`, while this repo (post-2026-08-09 rename) calls the same folder `tested-tools`. Neither side has updated to match the other. Not fixed here (fixing it means either renaming this repo's folder again or editing a live Jarvis note — both out of scope for a research-only pass), just named so it doesn't get silently assumed-consistent later.

## 2. What script needs updating

Not the scripts' logic — nothing found in this pass suggests `sync-all.sh`, `sync-jarvis.sh`, `check_dependency.py`, or `update-sandbox.sh` are currently broken. What needs updating is the **documentation describing them**, which undersells or misdescribes what's actually live:

- `60_Claude/README.md`'s `scripts/` row just says "`check_dependency.py`... plus the Jarvis sync scripts" — doesn't name `sync-all.sh` as the live multi-project engine or `sync-jarvis.sh` as the legacy, kept-for-rollback-only predecessor. A reader has no way to tell which one actually runs today without reading `_docs/Sync.md`'s amendments in full.
- `_docs/Sync.md`'s main narrative (everything before the final 2026-08-10 amendment) is written entirely around `sync-jarvis.sh` as "the actual mechanism now in use" — true when written, superseded since. The multi-project rollout is mentioned only in the last amendment, easy to miss on a skim. Not rewritten in this pass (out of scope — no doc content was to be authored beyond what directly answers this audit and the Jarvis-context updates), but flagged as the next thing to fix if `_docs/Sync.md` gets touched again.
- No script exists yet to mechanically verify the `instructions/`/`tests/`/`tested-tools/_future/` scope described below — none is needed yet either, since none of those folders has real content to check.

## 3. What template needs updating

`60_Claude/Templates/` holds 10 templates: 7 generic PARA vault-note templates (`area-note`, `daily-note`, `idea-note`, `literature-note`, `meeting-note`, `person-note`, `project-note`) and 3 AI-session-artifact templates (`pattern-note`, `session-log`, `weekly-summary`). All 10 are generic and self-consistent — nothing found in this pass says any of them is factually wrong.

The gap is a missing template, not a wrong one: **`60_Claude/Templates/weekly-summary.md`'s shape (What Moved / Wins / Blockers / Repeated Ideas / Open Loops / Next Week's Top 3) has no relationship to the actual review shape Jarvis just built** — `30_Order/Templates/Capability/AI Tools Weekly Review Template.md`'s real sections are Period Covered, Sources Reviewed (a checklist naming which real logs were opened), What Ran This Period, Sync & Capture Health (real OK/failed counts), Findings, Decided Fixes (gated — only 100%-clarity items), Open Questions, Next Period's Watch List. These serve genuinely different subjects (this repo's own engineering-session rollups vs. Jarvis's usage/sync-health reviews), so `weekly-summary.md` isn't wrong for its own purpose — but if this repo ever wants its own equivalent of Jarvis's citation-disciplined review template (e.g. to review this repo's own `sandbox/`→`tested-tools/` pipeline activity), no template here currently provides that shape. Not added in this pass — naming the gap, not building it.

No template exists anywhere in this repo for the new `tested-tools/_future/<repo>/FOR-WHAT.md` pattern Anant described 2026-08-19. That pattern is meant to repeat identically across every parked repo, which is exactly the kind of thing a template should back — currently nothing does.

## 4. Vault-rules are thin — what's missing

`60_Claude/vault-rules/` (`folder-structure.md`, `naming-conventions.md`, `linking-strategy.md`, `tagging-system.md`) covers exactly one subject well: the generic PARA note-taking layer (`00_Daily/`, `10_Areas/`, `20_Projects/`, `30_Knowledge/`, `40_Career/`). It covers **zero** subjects specific to this repo's actual reason for existing — the qualification pipeline itself. This is the literal thinness: `CLAUDE.md` tells anyone drafting in `agents/`, `commands/`, `hooks/`, `skills/` to "read `60_Claude/vault-rules/` first," but nothing in that folder says anything about how those staging folders, `sandbox/`, or `tested-tools/` should be named, structured, or organized. That knowledge currently exists only in `tested-tools/README.md`, `_docs/Architecture.md`, and `_docs/Promotion-Criteria.md` — none of which is `vault-rules/`, and none of which a fresh session is pointed at by the same instruction that names `vault-rules/`.

Concretely missing, confirmed absent from all four existing files:
- Any naming/structure convention for `sandbox/<repo-name>/` (currently just "whatever `git clone` produces" — true today, but never actually stated as the rule).
- The `tested-tools/<type>/<use-case>/<repo>/` three-level convention, which exists only in `tested-tools/README.md` today — not cross-referenced from `vault-rules/` at all, despite being exactly the kind of structural convention that folder exists to hold.
- Any convention for the top-level staging folders (`agents/`, `commands/`, `hooks/`, `skills/`) beyond "draft here, promote later" — no naming scheme, no per-source-repo subfolder convention, nothing.
- The brand-new `instructions/` folder (per-codebase subfolder holding `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`/`PRD.md`-shaped files) and `tests/` folder (the gate proving a tool's usefulness before promotion) — both scoped for the first time 2026-08-19 (see `_docs/Repo-Map.md`), neither has any convention written anywhere yet, `vault-rules/` included.
- The `tested-tools/_future/<repo>/FOR-WHAT.md` pattern — same story: scoped 2026-08-19, no convention written down.

**Net assessment:** `vault-rules/` is not wrong, it's answering a question ("how do I write a good vault note") that isn't this repo's central question ("how do I stage, test, and promote a piece of external tooling"). A fifth file — something like `60_Claude/vault-rules/pipeline-conventions.md` — is the natural home for all five missing conventions above, so that "read `60_Claude/vault-rules/` first" actually covers what a session drafting in `agents/`, `commands/`, `hooks/`, `skills/`, `instructions/`, or `tested-tools/_future/` needs. Not written in this pass — this doc only names the gap, per instruction to research and document, not build.

**Key finding that shapes Phase 1 of the Claudekit prompt:** `commands/compress.md`/`preserve.md`/`resume.md` were added in a commit explicitly titled "CPR commands" — they implement the exact pattern of a real external repo (`cpr-compress-preserve-resume`) tracked in Jarvis's own install lists, but that repo was **never actually cloned or tested**. That's a live instance of the exact failure this pipeline exists to prevent, just self-inflicted in this repo's own scaffold. Everything else in `agents/`, `commands/`, `hooks/` traces to the original scaffold commit with no external-repo origin at all — the prompt instructs the session to verify this via `git log`, not assume it, and to ask you (batched, one question) how to handle the zero-provenance set rather than fabricate a source.

**Jarvis prompt** (paste into a fresh Windows session, cwd = Jarvis vault): fills in the actually-empty `Review System.md` from the real 2026-08-10/11 build, reconciles the `tested-skills`/`tested-tools` terminology drift, fixes the three stale tool tables already flagged by Jarvis's own notes, resolves the empty `Cursor.md` stub, and logs what changed. **3. ## Vault-wide logging convention (for context)

Three files (`Claude Kit/Log.md`, `Write Log.md`, main `Session Log.md`) all explicitly claim the same `## [YYYY-MM-DD] tag | title` heading convention and describe themselves as "append-only." That reasoning was correct for `CLAUDE.md` specifically, but incomplete: it never considered `_docs/*.md` (PRD, Design, Architecture, Promotion-Criteria, Sync, Jarvis, Repo-Map, Gaps, plus the two new archives), which govern this repo just as much and currently sync to Jarvis **nowhere at all**. All 10 entries are already marked `status: live`.

Syncing `skills/` live into real projects would mean unvetted candidates flowing into live project configs, which is exactly what the qualification pipeline exists to prevent. I'm having the codebase prompt confirm this with you explicitly rather than guess.
